g2 is on the diagonal. g3 is not.

If ♛ is white, White has a Queen and a Pawn. This is a winning position.

This means the King MUST move.
This proves the King is in check.
The only piece that can check the King at h3 (given the board) is the piece at h1.
Therefore, ♛ at h1 must be a Black Queen.
So White's only pieces are the King (h3) and a pawn (h4).
Black has a King (g7), two Rooks (f8, h5), a Knight (b6), and two pawns (g6, h6), and a Queen (h1).
White is completely lost. The goal is to prolong the game or hope for a stalemate.
Moving the king to g4 or g3.
g4 puts the king on the 4th rank.
g3 puts the king on the 3rd rank.
If I move to g4, it might be easier to get checked by the Rook at h5 (which controls the 5th rank, but the king is on the 4th).
If I move to g3, it's more conservative.
However, in a losing position, I'll just pick one. h3g4 seems slightly more active.
